# Function: Fare\_DisplayFaresForCityPair

* * *

## 1 Overview

The function DisplayFaresForCityPair returns a list of available fares on a given city pair. It also returns limited information on the rules applied for each fare. The Fare Notes service can be used as a subsequent transaction to display the full rule associated to a fare.

## 1.1 Supported Operations

The following options may be used:

-   One or multiple carrier(s)
-   Date of the travel (travel commencement date, range of travel commencement dates, exact outbound date, exact inbound date, )
-   Shopper Fare Display (all carriers)
-   Various Fare Display options to limit the number of fares in the response  
    
    -   Unsalable fares only,
    -   Half round-trip fares only,
    -   Round-trip fares only,
    -   One-way fares only,
    -   up to 3 request type codes
    
    -   Specific Booking code only
    
    -   Expanded parameters
    -   Specific Cabin only
    -   Only fare filed in a specific currency
    -   Specific Global direction (for example Eastern Hemisphere)
    -   Filter fares by Tour code
-   Add taxes 
-   Unifare and corporate requests (up to 6 corporate names or numbers)
-   Miles and cash
-   Display fare family name
-   Display estimated points accrual

All dates must be specified in format DDMMYY, e.g. 10 March 2008 is 100308.  Dates may be future or past, and nermally travel dates would be 'future'. When ticket date is specified, normally it would be in the past since what data will be available at a future ticket date cannot be predicted. Please refer to 'Limitations' below for acceptability of dates.

Processing default for WBS request returning fares in structured format is to always order the fares by reference to fare amount from lowest (at the beginning of the message) to higher (at the end of the message).

Return of structured data provides the ability to the service user when displaying the data to apply any criteria they wish in ordering their display, without necessarily being bound by the order in which the data is returned in the reply message.

Similarly, if they wish, the service users may construct a simpler display for their users by omitting certain data, (e.g. rule data), from the display they build

Users wishing to have a pre-formatted display identical to the cryptic response could simply use cryptic request/response.

## 1.2 Limitations

This service is supported by the same data processing and functionality as the equivalent cryptic request and response and is therefore subject to the same limitations, (with the exception of the one or two options identified as supported only for web service and unsupported for cryptic requests.

For example, on cryptic a display limitation applies of up to 200 fares on 270 display lines. Dependent on setting of switch D31, this limitation may also apply to WBS requests for data in structured format, or up to 400 fares may be returned, (with no corresponding limitation on number of 'display lines' since the concept does not apply to structured format).

Application of default processes are by reference to switch settings defined for the office id and cannot be set to impact cryptic and WBS requests differently except when included in the design of the switch as in D31.

Limitation on the acceptability of dates are as follows:

-   Travel date specified may up to one year in the past
-   Travel date specified may be up to 730 days into the future
-   Ticketing date specified may be up to two years into the past
-   If ticketing date is specified and is in the future from system date, processing will use data stored in the database at the time the request is made. No guarantee can be provided that data to build the response will be the same if request is repeated at that future date.

## 1.3 Unsupported Operations

None

## 1.4 Prerequisites

None

## 2 Building A Query

**Date information**

Date information can be provided in 3 different ways:

-   Specific travel commencement date (no qualifier)
-   Range of travel commencement dates (qualifiers A and B)
-   Outbound and inbound date (qualifiers O and I)

In case of range of dates or outbound and inbound dates, 2 dates must be provided:

-   in case of range of dates, one date must have qualifier B (beginning of range of travel commencement dates) and the other one must have qualifier A (end of travel commencement date range)
-   in case of outbound and inbound dates, one date must have qualifier O (outbound) and the other must have qualifier I (Inbound)

**Carrier information**

It is possible to specify up to 3 airline codes.

It is also possible to exclude neutral common (YY) fares (which may be returned by default in a separate group if the requsted carrier participates in them)

To request return of YY fares only if a specified carrier participates

To demand return of fares from all airlines publishing fares on the requested city pair, (shopper display).

## 3 Receiving A Reply

The service returns:

-   Information messages such as:
    -   the list of carriers who may have oublished fares on the city pair
    -   the list of carriers who do not participate in YY fares,
    -   tax information,
    -   surcharge information,
    -   airport information,
-   a list of fare groups.

Each fare group contains:

-   A rate of exchange, Rounding Type and Rounding Unit for fares of the currency of country of the origin city of the city pair requested,
-   TPM/MPM, \*when existing)
-   A global direction (Ex: Within Eastern Hemisphere, ...),
-   A Request Type code (Ex: NSP),
-   A Carrier,
-   A City pair,
-   Dates requested and processed,
-   Routing number, (when applicable)
-   Free texts,
-   Corporate name,
-   A list of fares.

For each fare, the service returns:

-   An item number,
-   A carrier code,
-   A fare basis,
-   A booking code,
-   Fare type indicators, (private, corporate, negotiated, etc)
-   Monetary information, (currency code)
-   Fare qualifiers,
-   Penalty information, Dates/Days, Advance purchase restrictions, Minimum and maximum stay restrictions,
-   routing type or number .

2.1.1. Fare Qualifier

\- Penalty :

798 : subject to cancel penalties (+ amount )

740 : percentage (+percentage)

741 : not refundable

736 : refundable (+ check rule)

Default: no penalty restriction

\- Min/Max stay

745: min stay in days (+ days)

746: max stay in days(+ days)

794: maximum stay in months (+ months)

MNR: min stay restrictions (+ Check rules)

MXR: max stay restrictions (+ Check rules)

MNM: min stay in months (+ months)

BMN: min stay more than 365 days

BMX: max stay more than 365 days

Default: No min max stay restriction

\- Advance purchase

702 : advances purchase period  (+ days + check rules)

Default: no advance purchase restriction

 - Days

726 : Fare restricted to certain days (+ days + check rules (hours, …)

Default: no day restriction

\- Seasonality

766 : seasonality (+Dates)

767 : seasonality on outbound (+Dates)

768:  seasonality on inbound (+Dates)

TBT: trip by trip indicator

Default: no seasonality restriction

 - Sales restriction

775 : TICKET ONLY ON THIS DATE

776 : TICKET ON OR AFTER THIS DATE

777 : TICKET ON OR BEFORE THIS DATE

Default: no sales restriction

782 : COMPLETE ALL TRAVEL BY THIS DATE

## 4 Error Messages

In case of error, 2 information are returned:

-   an Error code in errorInfo/rejectErrorCode/errorDetails/errorCode
-   an Error text in errorInfo/errorFreeText/freeText

## 4.1 Error Reply

<Fare\_DisplayFaresForCityPairReply xmlns="http://xml.amadeus.com/FQDRES\_12\_1\_1A"> <transactionType> <messageFunctionDetails> <messageFunction>711</messageFunction> </messageFunctionDetails> </transactionType> <errorInfo> <rejectErrorCode> <errorDetails> <errorCode>0</errorCode> </errorDetails> </rejectErrorCode> <errorFreeText> <freeText>UNKNOWN CITY/AIRPORT</freeText> </errorFreeText> </errorInfo> </Fare\_DisplayFaresForCityPairReply>

## 5 Operations

## 5.1 Operation: 01 Origin and Destination City/Airport

This example shows a query with origin and destination city/airport provided:

-   Origin : PAR
-   Destination : LON

The service returns:

-   Information messages such as the list of carriers for the itinerary, the list of carriers who do not participate in YY, tax information, surcharge information
-   One fare group

The fare group contains:

-   the rate of exchange, Rounding Type and Rounding Unit for the Country of the origin city of the city pair requested
-   TPM/MPM
-   the global direction (7EH : Within Eastern Hemisphere (TC2 and TC3), except for PO and TS)
-   Default Request Type code (NSP)
-   Carrier (YY)
-   City pair (PAR, LON)
-   Validity dates (for travel commencement in line with request)
-   4 fares

For each fare the service returns:

-   Item number (01, 02, 03, 04)
-   Booking code (No info available - Check rules (742))
-   carrier (YY)
-   fare basis (CIF, YIF)
-   fare type indicator (724: Fare is mileage base)
-   monetary information (One way and/or Round trip amount, Fees amount, Tax amount)

Corresponding cryptic : FQD PAR LON

## 5.1.3 Possible Errors

See "Error Messages" section.

* * *

## 5.2 Operation: 02 Carrier information - Specific airline and common fares

This example shows a query with origin, destination and a specific airline provided:

-   Origin : LON
-   Destination : NYC
-   Airline : IB

The service returns 2 fare groups: one for airline IB and, (because system finds that IB is a participant in YY fares), one for neutral fares (YY).

Because no date is specified, system defaults to display fares that are valid for travel at system date

Corresponding cryptic : FQD LON NYC /AIB

## 5.2.1 Query Structure

![](/resources/externalFiles/images/userguideeditor/icon_collapse.gif)XML Example

## 5.2.3 Possible Errors

See "Error Messages" section.

* * *

## 5.3 Operation: 03 Carrier information - Specific airline only (exclude common fares)

This example shows a query with origin, destination, specific airline and no IATA common fare provided:

-   Origin : LON
-   Destination : NYC
-   Airline : IB
-   no IATA common fares : NYY

The service returns one fare group for airline IB.

Corresponding cryptic : FQD LON NYC /A-IB

Again, because no date is specified in  the request, system displays fares that are valid for travel at system date

## 5.3.1 Query Structure

![](/resources/externalFiles/images/userguideeditor/icon_collapse.gif)XML Example

## 5.3.3 Possible Errors

See "Error Messages" section.

* * *

## 5.4 Operation: 04 Carrier information - Multiple airlines

This example shows a query with origin, destination and three airlines:

-   Origin : PAR
-   Destination : LON
-   Airlines : LH, AF, BA

The service returns the fares from the three airlines.

Corresponding cryptic : FQD LON NYC /ALH,AF,BA

Because the fares of multiple carriers have been requested, reply structure is in same format as 'shopper' display

## 5.4.1 Query Structure

![](/resources/externalFiles/images/userguideeditor/icon_collapse.gif)XML Example

<Fare\_DisplayFaresForCityPair xmlns="http://xml.amadeus.com/FQDREQ\_12\_1\_1A"> <msgType> <messageFunctionDetails> <messageFunction>711</messageFunction> </messageFunctionDetails> </msgType> <transportInformation> <transportService> <companyIdentification> <marketingCompany>LH</marketingCompany> <marketingCompany>AF</marketingCompany> <marketingCompany>BA</marketingCompany> </companyIdentification> </transportService> </transportInformation> <tripDescription> <origDest> <origin>PAR</origin> <destination>LON</destination> </origDest> </tripDescription> </Fare\_DisplayFaresForCityPair>

## 5.5 Operation: 05 Carrier information - Common fare only

This example shows a query with origin, destination and common fares only:

-   Origin : LON
-   Destination : NYC
-   Common fares : YY

The service returns one fare group for common fares (YY).

Corresponding cryptic : FQD LON NYC /AYY

* * *

## 5.6 Operation: 06 Date information - Specific date

This example shows a query with origin and destination city/airport and a specific travel date

-   Origin : LON
-   Destination : NYC
-   Specific date : 14AUG11

Use of specific travel commencement date overrides system default to use system date as travel commencement date.  Cryptic equivalent - FQD LONNYC/D14AUG11

## 5.6.1 Query Structure

![](/resources/externalFiles/images/userguideeditor/icon_collapse.gif)XML Example

<Fare\_DisplayFaresForCityPair xmlns="http://xml.amadeus.com/FQDREQ\_12\_1\_1A"> <msgType> <messageFunctionDetails> <messageFunction>711</messageFunction> </messageFunctionDetails> </msgType> <tripDescription> <origDest> <origin>LON</origin> <destination>NYC</destination> </origDest> <dateFlightMovement> <dateAndTimeDetails> <date>140811</date> </dateAndTimeDetails> </dateFlightMovement> </tripDescription> </Fare\_DisplayFaresForCityPair>

## 5.6.2 Reply Structure

![](/resources/externalFiles/images/userguideeditor/icon_collapse.gif)XML Example

## 5.6.3 Possible Errors

See "Error Messages" section.

* * *

## 5.7 Operation: 07 Date information - Exact outbound and inbound travel date

This example shows a query with origin and destination city/airport and outbound and inbound travel date

-   Origin : LON
-   Destination : NYC
-   Outbound date : 27JUL11
-   Inbound date : 15AUG11

Corresponding cryptic : FQD LON NYC /D27JUL11\*15AUG11

System processing responds to this date format by eliminating from display any fares not valid for use in pricing at system date for the chosen dates. Fares may be invalidated by ref to advance purchase conditions or minimum or maximum stay restrictions

## 5.7.1 Query Structure

![](/resources/externalFiles/images/userguideeditor/icon_collapse.gif)XML Example

<Fare\_DisplayFaresForCityPair xmlns="http://xml.amadeus.com/FQDREQ\_12\_1\_1A"> <msgType> <messageFunctionDetails> <messageFunction>711</messageFunction> </messageFunctionDetails> </msgType> <tripDescription> <origDest> <origin>LON</origin> <destination>NYC</destination> </origDest> <dateFlightMovement> <dateAndTimeDetails> <qualifier>O</qualifier> <date>270711</date> </dateAndTimeDetails> <dateAndTimeDetails> <qualifier>I</qualifier> <date>150811</date> </dateAndTimeDetails> </dateFlightMovement> </tripDescription> </Fare\_DisplayFaresForCityPair>

## 5.7.2 Reply Structure

![](/resources/externalFiles/images/userguideeditor/icon_collapse.gif)XML Example

## 5.7.3 Possible Errors

See "Error Messages" section.

* * *

## 5.8 Operation: 08 Date information - Range of travel start dates

This example shows a query with origin and destination city/airport and range of travel commencement dates

-   Origin : LON
-   Destination : NYC
-   Travel date range : 27JUL11-15AUG11

Corresponding cryptic : FQD LON NYC /D27JUL11\*\*15AUG11

Any fare not valid for travel commencement within the date range will be invalidated for inclusion in the reply

## 5.8.1 Query Structure

![](/resources/externalFiles/images/userguideeditor/icon_collapse.gif)XML Example

<Fare\_DisplayFaresForCityPair xmlns="http://xml.amadeus.com/FQDREQ\_12\_1\_1A"> <msgType> <messageFunctionDetails> <messageFunction>711</messageFunction> </messageFunctionDetails> </msgType> <tripDescription> <origDest> <origin>LON</origin> <destination>NYC</destination> </origDest> <dateFlightMovement> <dateAndTimeDetails> <qualifier>B</qualifier> <date>270711</date> </dateAndTimeDetails> <dateAndTimeDetails> <qualifier>A</qualifier> <date>150811</date> </dateAndTimeDetails> </dateFlightMovement> </tripDescription> </Fare\_DisplayFaresForCityPair>

## 5.8.2 Reply Structure

![](/resources/externalFiles/images/userguideeditor/icon_collapse.gif)XML Example

## 5.8.3 Possible Errors

See "Error Messages" section.

* * *

## 5.9 Operation: 09 Different start of travel and ticketing date

Example of request with :

Ticketing date: 27May11

Travel date: 14July11

Corresponding cryptic : FQD LONNYC/D14JUL11/R,27MAY11

Ticket date is assumed in processing to be equal to system date unless specified in the request. Fares valid for travel on the travel commencement date requested but filed after the specified ticket date are invalidated from display.

## 5.9.1 Query Structure

![](/resources/externalFiles/images/userguideeditor/icon_collapse.gif)XML Example

<Fare\_DisplayFaresForCityPair xmlns="http://xml.amadeus.com/FQDREQ\_12\_1\_1A"> <msgType> <messageFunctionDetails> <messageFunction>711</messageFunction> </messageFunctionDetails> </msgType> <pricingTickInfo> <productDateTimeDetails> <ticketingDate>070711</ticketingDate> </productDateTimeDetails> </pricingTickInfo> <tripDescription> <origDest> <origin>LON</origin> <destination>NYC</destination> </origDest> <dateFlightMovement> <dateAndTimeDetails> <date>140811</date> </dateAndTimeDetails> </dateFlightMovement> </tripDescription> </Fare\_DisplayFaresForCityPair>

## 5.9.2 Reply Structure

![](/resources/externalFiles/images/userguideeditor/icon_collapse.gif)XML Example

## 5.9.3 Possible Errors

See "Error Messages" section.

* * *

## 5.10 Operation: 10 Booking code

Only one booking code can be specified.

Example of query with:

Origin : LON

Destination : NYC

booking code : Y

The service returns the fares with Y as booking code.

Corresponding cryptic : FQD LON NYC /CY

Since no carrier is specified, system defaults to return YY fares with published prime booking code of 'Y'

## 5.10.1 Query Structure

![](/resources/externalFiles/images/userguideeditor/icon_collapse.gif)XML Example

<Fare\_DisplayFaresForCityPair xmlns="http://xml.amadeus.com/FQDREQ\_12\_1\_1A"> <msgType> <messageFunctionDetails> <messageFunction>711</messageFunction> </messageFunctionDetails> </msgType> <availCabinConf> <productDetailsQualifier>SB</productDetailsQualifier> <bookingClassDetails> <designator>Y</designator> </bookingClassDetails> </availCabinConf> <tripDescription> <origDest> <origin>LON</origin> <destination>NYC</destination> </origDest> </tripDescription> </Fare\_DisplayFaresForCityPair>

## 5.10.2 Reply Structure

![](/resources/externalFiles/images/userguideeditor/icon_collapse.gif)XML Example

## 5.10.3 Possible Errors

See "Error Messages" section.

* * *

## 5.11 Operation: 11 Cabin

Up to 3 cabin can be specified in the request.

Example of query with:

Origin : LON

Destination : NYC

Carrier : LH

Cabin : F (First)

Corresponding cryptic : FQD LON NYC /ALH /KF

Fares for the city pair and default travel date are selected by reference to the booking codes defined as associated to the cabin for the requested carrier.

Other cabin values are

-   C = Business
-   W = Premium economy
-   M = non-premium economy
-   Y =  economy, (both premium and non-premium)

## 5.11.1 Query Structure

![](/resources/externalFiles/images/userguideeditor/icon_collapse.gif)XML Example

<Fare\_DisplayFaresForCityPair xmlns="http://xml.amadeus.com/FQDREQ\_12\_1\_1A"> <msgType> <messageFunctionDetails> <messageFunction>711</messageFunction> </messageFunctionDetails> </msgType> <availCabinConf> <productDetailsQualifier>SC</productDetailsQualifier> <bookingClassDetails> <designator>F</designator> </bookingClassDetails> </availCabinConf> <transportInformation> <transportService> <companyIdentification> <marketingCompany>LH</marketingCompany> </companyIdentification> </transportService> </transportInformation> <tripDescription> <origDest> <origin>LON</origin> <destination>NYC</destination> </origDest> </tripDescription> </Fare\_DisplayFaresForCityPair>

## 5.11.2 Reply Structure

![](/resources/externalFiles/images/userguideeditor/icon_collapse.gif)XML Example

## 5.11.3 Possible Errors

See "Error Messages" section.

* * *

## 5.12 Operation: 12 Shopper display - display fares for all Amadeus Participating Airlines

Example of Shopper display with:

-   Origin : LON
-   Destination : NYC
-   Reply includes list of carriers participating (or not) in IATA fares 

Corresponding cryptic request: FQD LON NYC /S

-   Processing default for this cryptic request is to return the fare display with fares ordered from lowest to highest amount, (usual default for single carrier display is to display from highest to lowest).

## 5.12.1 Query Structure

![](/resources/externalFiles/images/userguideeditor/icon_collapse.gif)XML Example

## 5.13 Operation: 13 Fare options - One-way fares

In the query, we can specify the following fare options:

-   One-way fares only
-   Round-trip fares only
-   Half round-trip fares only (with display defaulting to NUC)
-   Unsaleable fares only (i.e. fares not valid for sale at point making the request)
-   Carrier Fares only, no IATA common fares
-   IATA fares in which up to 3 specific carriers participate
-   Add tax
-   Withhold tax
-   Add fees
-   Withhold fees
-   Swedish VAT (in Swedish domestic)
-   All Date restrictions
-   Asean Extension fares

In this example, we request one-way fares only (756).

Only one way fares will be returned by the service.

Corresponding cryptic : FQD LON NYC /IO

Default carrier, travel date and request type applies

## 5.14 Operation: 14 Fare options - Add all Automated Fees

This example shows a query with :

-   Origin : PAR
-   Destination : FRA
-   Airline : LH
-   Booking code : Y
-   Add all Automated Fees : AOB

The service returns :

-   an information message (OB FEES INCLUDED)
-   and for each fare the following amounts:

-   OB fees amount for One Way : O00
-   OB fees amount for Round Trip : O01

Corresponding cryptic :FQD PAR FRA /CY /ALH /R,FA

## 5.17 Operation: 17 Fare options - Add Taxes

The service option allows to add Taxes if not included by default. However, users should be aware that inclusion of taxes, whether by default or specific request, results in a purely informative display which is frequently inaccurate when compared with pricing of the same fares. This is due to the limitations of the informative fare display, e.g. single tax calculation applied to all fares selected for display, also potential intermediate points and flight numbers visible in pricing are not available in fare display tax calculation.

This example shows a query with :

-   Origin : LON
-   Destination : NYC
-   Airline : LH
-   Booking code : Y
-   Add Taxes : 707

The service returns :

-   an information message (TAX INCLUDED) and the list of included taxexs (AY, GB, XA, XY, YC, YQ)
-   and for each fare the following amounts:

-   Total taxes amount for One Way : T00
-   Total taxes for Round Trip : T01

Corresponding cryptic : FQD LON NYC /ALH /CY /R,AT

## 5.17.1 Query Structure

![](/resources/externalFiles/images/userguideeditor/icon_collapse.gif)XML Example

## 5.18 Operation: 18 Currency - Fares converted in NUC

This example shows a query with :

-   Origin : LON
-   Destination : NYC

## 5.18.3 Possible Errors

* * *

## 5.19 Operation: 19 Currency - Fares filed in a foreign currency

This example shows a query with origin:

-   Origin : MEX
-   Destination : ACA
-   Airline : AM
-   Foreign currency : MXN (actually currency of country of origin, but limited to domestic fares)

The service returns the fares filed in MXN currency.

Corresponding cryptic : FQD MEX ACA /AAM /R,FS-MXN

This option is only needed to request display of fares filed in a currency other than the default currency of country of origin, i.e. when currency default must be overidden.

## 5.19.1 Query Structure

![](/resources/externalFiles/images/userguideeditor/icon_collapse.gif)XML Example

## 5.20 Operation: 20 Request type codes

Up to 3 request type codes can be requested.

This example shows a query with :

-   Origin : LON
-   Destination : NYC
-   Airlines : BA
-   Booking code : S
-   Request type codes : APX, PEX and MIL

Corresponding cryptic : FQD LON NYC /AAB /CB /R,-APX-PEX-MIL

Request type code target the display of specific fares and sometimes, due to associated programmed default, override default currency of display, e.g. request for 'MIL' may cause system to default to US military fares filed in USD. Should there be no match between the specified request type and filed data, the request will fail, consequently special request should be used in automated processes with caution.

List of request type codes  types can be found in Altea with the cryptic entry 'HE FARE REQ'

## 5.21 Operation: 21 Expanded parameters list

This example shows a query with :

-   Origin : LON
-   Destination : NYC
-   Airline : BA
-   No Advance Purchase : NAP
-   No Penalty : NPE

The service returns fares with advance purchase and no penalty.

Corresponding cryptic : FQD LON NYC /ABA /R,\*NAP-NPE

THE EXPANDED PARAMETER CODES ARE AS FOLLOWS:         
                                                     
      CODE          MEANING                          
      ----          -------                          
      EU            ECONOMY UNRESTRICTED ONLY        
      NAP           NO ADVANCE PURCHASE INFORMATION  
      NDA           NO DAY OR TIME INFORMATION       
      NMX           NO MAXIMUM STAY INFORMATION      
      NMN           NO MINIMUM STAY INFORMATION      
      NPE           NO PENALTY INFORMATION           
      PE            PENALTY INFORMATION              
      NR            NO RESTRICTION                   
      NMM           NO MINIMUM AND NO MAXIMUM FARE   
      NRF           NON-REFUNDABLE FARES             
      RF            REFUNDABLE FARES

## 5.22 Operation: 22 Global direction

The global direction can be specified in the query.

Example of query with:

Origin : LON

Destination : BKK

Global direction : TS (Trans-Siberian route)

The service returns the fares with TS as global direction.

Corresponding cryptic : FQD LON BKK /VTS

Specification of global direction is only required when fares have been filed by the requested carrier on more than one global direction and requester is only interested in viewing the fare applicable to one global direction.  For example between SouthWest Pacific and Europe at least three possible global directions are in common use. For Sydney to London passengers can travel on EH (Eastern Hemisphere) e.g. SYD-BOM-LON, or on TS (Trans Siberian) e.g. SYD-TYO-LON or AP (Atlantic/Pacific) e.g. SYD-LAX-LON.

If requester wishes to only propose the fare of a single global direction, this option should be used

## 5.22.1 Query Structure

![](/resources/externalFiles/images/userguideeditor/icon_collapse.gif)XML Example

<Fare\_DisplayFaresForCityPair xmlns="http://xml.amadeus.com/FQDREQ\_12\_1\_1A"> <msgType> <messageFunctionDetails> <messageFunction>711</messageFunction> </messageFunctionDetails> </msgType> <flightQualification> <movementType>7TS</movementType> </flightQualification> <tripDescription> <origDest> <origin>LON</origin> <destination>BKK</destination> </origDest> </tripDescription> </Fare\_DisplayFaresForCityPair>

## 5.23 Operation: 23 Type of fare - Unifare and Public fares

This example shows a query with :

-   Origin : LON
-   Destination : NYC
-   Unifare fares : RU
-   Public fares : RP

The service returns Unifare and Public Fares.

Corresponding cryptic : FQD LON NYC /R,UP

'Public Fares' are filed with unrestricted distribution in the public tariff

'Unifares' is the term used by Amadeus to identify all other fares that have restricted distribution and filedin private tariff, i.e. may only be sold by specific agents.  These are 'private'(category 15) and negotiated (category 35) fares from any sources (ATPCO, SITA or Amadeus).

## 5.23.1 Query Structure

![](/resources/externalFiles/images/userguideeditor/icon_collapse.gif)XML Example

<Fare\_DisplayFaresForCityPair xmlns="http://xml.amadeus.com/FQDREQ\_12\_1\_1A"> <msgType> <messageFunctionDetails> <messageFunction>711</messageFunction> </messageFunctionDetails> </msgType> <pricingTickInfo> <priceTicketDetails> <indicators>RU</indicators> <indicators>RP</indicators> </priceTicketDetails> </pricingTickInfo> <tripDescription> <origDest> <origin>LON</origin> <destination>NYC</destination> </origDest> </tripDescription> </Fare\_DisplayFaresForCityPair>

![](/resources/externalFiles/images/userguideeditor/icon_collapse.gif)XML Example

## 5.24 Operation: 24 Corporate contract name

This example shows a query with :

-   Origin : FRA
-   Destination : BER
-   Airline : LH
-   Booking code : Y
-   Corporate name : SIEMENS

FQD FRA BER /ALH /CY /R,U\*SIEMENS

Corporate name is an extension of the distribution security of Unifares and may be 1-10 alphanumerics. A corporate number is filed and an alpha numeric mnemonic name is created. This is intended as an aid to cryptic users, (a name is easier to remember than a number). The request may use either corporate name or number.

## 5.25 Operation: 25 Corporate contract number

Up to 6 corporate codes or names can be specified.

This example shows a query with :

Origin : PAR

Destination : NCE

List of corporate codes : 000001-000002-000003-000004-000005-000006

Corresponding cryptic : FQD PAR NCE /R,U000001-000002-000003-000004-000005-000006

Corporate number is an extension of the distribution security of Unifares. A corporate number is filed and an equivalent alpha numeric mnemonic name may be created in Amadeus. The request may use either corporate name or number.

## 5.25.1 Query Structure

![](/resources/externalFiles/images/userguideeditor/icon_collapse.gif)XML Example

<Fare\_DisplayFaresForCityPair xmlns="http://xml.amadeus.com/FQDREQ\_12\_1\_1A"> <msgType> <messageFunctionDetails> <messageFunction>711</messageFunction> </messageFunctionDetails> </msgType> <multiCorporate> <corporateId> <corporateQualifier>RU</corporateQualifier> <identity>000001</identity> <identity>000002</identity> <identity>000003</identity> <identity>000004</identity> <identity>000005</identity> <identity>000006</identity> </corporateId> </multiCorporate> <tripDescription> <origDest> <origin>PAR</origin> <destination>NCE</destination> </origDest> </tripDescription> </Fare\_DisplayFaresForCityPair>

## 5.26 Operation: 26 Service Fee Management with PSR number

The SFM must be calculated with the provided Pricing Scheme Reference.

Example of query with:

Origin : PAR

Destination : LON

PS Number : PS and 12345678

Corresponding cryptic: FQD PAR LON /PS-12345678.

![](/resources/externalFiles/images/userguideeditor/icon_collapse.gif)XML Example

## 5.27 Operation: 27 Service Fee Management without PSR number

The SFM must be calculated with the default Pricing Scheme Reference.

Example of query with:

Origin : PAR

Destination : LON

Default PS Number : PS

Corresponding cryptic: FQD PAR LON /PS.

## 5.28 Operation: 28 Award

This option is for use with the Amadeus design for Award fares with 'USD as miles'. In this model, fares are filed via category 35 with corporate code.  Mileage amount of the Award is filed as an USD net fare amount with a selling fare of zero local currency. 

When the option is used, the specified carrier is selected as publishing carrier of the fare and the Net USD amount is automatically displayed. Combination of the request with corporate code is mandatory.

Cryptic example : FQD PAR LON /MLH/R,U000001

## 5.28.1 Query Structure

![](/resources/externalFiles/images/userguideeditor/icon_collapse.gif)XML Example

<Fare\_DisplayFaresForCityPair xmlns="http://xml.amadeus.com/FQDREQ\_12\_1\_1A"> <msgType> <messageFunctionDetails> <messageFunction>711</messageFunction> </messageFunctionDetails> </msgType> <transportInformation> <transportService> <companyIdentification> <marketingCompany>LH</marketingCompany> </companyIdentification> </transportService> <selectionDetail> <selectionDetails> <option>AI</option> </selectionDetails> </selectionDetail> </transportInformation> <tripDescription> <origDest> <origin>PAR</origin> <destination>LON</destination> </origDest> </tripDescription> </Fare\_DisplayFaresForCityPair>

## 5.28.2 Reply Structure

![](/resources/externalFiles/images/userguideeditor/icon_collapse.gif)XML Example

## 5.29 Operation: 29 Rule id

Example of request with rule id specified in the query.

-   Origin : PAR
-   Destination : NCE
-   Carrier : AF
-   One way fare : 756
-   Expanded parameter : Refundable fares
-   Booking code : W
-   Rule id : FRFF

Corresponding cryptic : FQD PAR NCE /AAF /CW /IO /R,RU-FRFF,\*RF

Options must be used with caution, e.g. to combine the '\*RF' expanded parameter requiring return of refundable fares with the selection of fares with rule number FRFF, the requester must be confident that the selected rule is refundable.  If the rule is not refundable, the use of the expanded parameter may cause the request to fail.

## 5.34 Operation: 34 Display fares for city pair reply 5

This example shows an outputfor a US/CAN domestic fare display.

The service returns for each fare the detail of the routing by city/airport code.

For fare 01 : ATL, BDL, BOS, CVG, DTT, FLL, IND, MEM, MSP, ORL, TPA, WAS.  
For fare 02 : ATL, BOS, CVG, DTT, MEM, ORL, RDU, WAS.

## 5.35 Operation: 35 Tour code filtering

The user has the ability to filter by tour code

This option is expected to be most useful to users when a high volume of fares is encountered in response to a request and it is already know that the fare to be reviewed bears a specific tour code, e.g. if it appears on a ticket but user wishes to display the fare in order to perform a subsequent request for rule display. Use of the option spefifying tour code then filters the fares of the request to select only the fare(s) bearing the tour code and to suppress all others.

Cryptic entry ; FQD SELNYC/AKE/R,U,TC-KETOUR

## 5.35.1 Query Structure

![](/resources/externalFiles/images/userguideeditor/icon_collapse.gif)XML Example

<Fare\_DisplayFaresForCityPair xmlns="http://xml.amadeus.com/FQDREQ\_12\_1\_1A"> <msgType> <messageFunctionDetails> <messageFunction>711</messageFunction> </messageFunctionDetails> </msgType> <tourInfo> <tourInformationDetails> <tourCode>KETOUR</tourCode> </tourInformationDetails> </tourInfo> <transportInformation> <transportService> <companyIdentification> <marketingCompany>KE</marketingCompany> </companyIdentification> </transportService> </transportInformation> <tripDescription> <origDest> <origin>SEL</origin> <destination>NYC</destination> </origDest> </tripDescription> </Fare\_DisplayFaresForCityPair>

## 5.35.2 Reply Structure

![](/resources/externalFiles/images/userguideeditor/icon_collapse.gif)XML Example

## 5.36 Operation: 36 Miles and Cash

The user has the ability to return Miles and cash amounts.

'Miles' are only applicable in frequent flyer award context when carrier chooses to use 'Miles and Cash' in preference to 'USD as Miles'

The user specifies in the query:

-   MC option
-   Frequent flyer info such as carrier, number, tier level and priority code

Miles and cash amounts are returned in the reply.

This option applies to structured requests only.  There is no equivalent cryptic entry

## 5.36.3 Possible Errors

See "Error Messages" section.

* * *

## 5.37 Operation: 37 Award fares per tier level

-   Carriers may define 'tier levels' to be applied in their frequent flyer award programme with different award mileage levels to be charged dependent on the tier level of the passenger.

Tier level definition is entirely at the discretion of the carrier.

Tier levels could be described as :

-   Bronze, silver, gold and platinum
-   Ivory silver and platimum
-   Pearl, sapphire, emerald and diamond
-   MK,  ML  and MM

Tier level specified for corporate award ; FQDICNCDG/M6X/R,U\*AWARD,TL-MM

## 5.37.1 Query Structure

![](/resources/externalFiles/images/userguideeditor/icon_collapse.gif)XML Example

<Fare\_DisplayFaresForCityPair xmlns="http://xml.amadeus.com/FQDREQ\_12\_1\_1A"> <msgType> <messageFunctionDetails> <messageFunction>711</messageFunction> </messageFunctionDetails> </msgType> <frequentFlyerInfo> <frequentTravellerDetails> <tierLevel>GOLD</tierLevel> </frequentTravellerDetails> </frequentFlyerInfo> <transportInformation> <transportService> <companyIdentification> <marketingCompany>LH</marketingCompany> </companyIdentification> </transportService> <selectionDetail> <selectionDetails> <option>AI</option> </selectionDetails> </selectionDetail> </transportInformation> <tripDescription> <origDest> <origin>PAR</origin> <destination>LON</destination> </origDest> </tripDescription> </Fare\_DisplayFaresForCityPair>

## 5.37.2 Reply Structure

![](/resources/externalFiles/images/userguideeditor/icon_collapse.gif)XML Example

## 5.37.3 Possible Errors

See "Error Messages" section.

* * *

## 5.38 Operation: 38 Display Fare Family

The user has the ability to display fare family names and rankings associated to the fares.

The user specifies in the query:

• FF option

• Carrier

In the reply, fare family names and rankings are displayed along with the returned fares if exist.

Corresponding cryptic: FQD TYO SPK/AJL/FF/17JUL/R, UP, FX, AT

## 5.39 Operation: 39 Display estimated points accrual

 The fare display output can be enabled to display the amount of airline points earned when purchasing the ticket.

The user specifies in the query:

• MA option

• Frequent Flyer airline program code
